What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess… my wife thought I should come in. I’ve been putting a lot into a memorial for my brother, and it’s kind of gotten bigger than I meant. Most days I’m calling people, finding songs, planning displays and trips for it. She says I’ve let the garden and my walking group and other things slide.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like every detail of the memorial has to be just right?\n\nPatient: I suppose so, yeah. It started as just a memorial, but now I keep chasing down songs, calling people, figuring out displays… even trips tied to it. Most days it takes over, and my wife says I’ve let the garden and walking group slip.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like if anything is not exactly as you envision it for the memorial, it would be a failure?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a failure. I just… I want it done properly. It matters, that’s all.\n\nDoctor: Has your focus on the memorial been causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has. I’ve missed my walking group and book club, and the garden’s gotten away from me. Some friends at the senior center got upset because I keep bringing everything back to the memorial display.\n\nMy wife says most of our days are just… her trying to calm me down or stop me from making another call, planning another trip, or getting into another argument about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things for the memorial, or would you say you want to do them?\n\nPatient: I’d say I want to… mostly. It’s for my brother, so it feels important. But it’s gotten bigger than I meant. I spend most days on songs, calls, displays, even planning trips for it, and then everything else sort of gets pushed aside.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to control how others remember your brother through this memorial?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about controlling how they remember him. I just… what brings me in is that it’s gotten bigger than I meant. I’m spending most days chasing down songs, calling people, planning displays and even trips. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden, my walking group… most everything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about the memorial even when you're trying to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. It jumps around on me. I’ll try to do something else, and then it’s the display case, or maybe going to find a cousin, or some line from a ballad. I can’t really slow it down enough to put it in order.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there's something wrong with you if the memorial isn't perfect?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d put it that way. I just… I want it done right. If it isn’t, it feels like I let him down, maybe. But “something wrong with me”… I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing all this for the memorial because you genuinely believe it's what your brother would have wanted, or because you feel like you owe it to him?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate those two. Maybe a little of both. I don’t really know what he would’ve wanted, not exactly. I just feel like… if I don’t do it, then I didn’t do enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you're not working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes. I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m guilty,” exactly. It’s more like I get uneasy if I’m not doing something for it. Then I try not to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could stop working on the memorial if you felt like you had done enough, or do you feel like you have to keep going no matter what?\n\nPatient: I guess… I don’t know where “enough” is. It started as a simple memorial, but now I’m spending most days on it—songs, calling people, planning displays, even trips. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and all that. I keep thinking I just need to get one more thing right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing all this work for the memorial because you couldn't live with yourself if it wasn't done?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d put it that strongly. I just… I get this bad feeling when I stop, like I’m leaving something unfinished. I try not to think past that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're driven to keep working on the memorial because you're trying to make it perfect, or because you feel obligated to your brother?\n\nPatient: Maybe both, I don’t know. It feels like I keep needing to do one more thing for him.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be able to stop working on the memorial if someone you trusted told you it was already perfect as it is?\n\nPatient: Maybe… if I really trusted them. But I don’t know. It’s just gotten so big now—most days I’m still chasing down songs or calling people or planning some display. Even trips. It’s hard to just stop, even if someone says it’s enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever consider the memorial complete if it wasn't perfect?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. Perfect’s a hard word. I just know when I try to call it done, I end up thinking of another song to find, or someone else to call, or some display to plan. It’s taken over more than I meant it to.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Most days I’m calling people, finding songs, planning displays and trips for it. She says I’ve let the garden and my walking group and other things slide.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like every detail of the memorial has to be just right?\n\nPatient: I suppose so, yeah. It started as just a memorial, but now I keep chasing down songs, calling people, figuring out displays… even trips tied to it. Most days it takes over, and my wife says I’ve let the garden and walking group slip.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like if anything is not exactly as you envision it for the memorial, it would be a failure?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a failure. I just… I want it done properly. It matters, that’s all.\n\nDoctor: Has your focus on the memorial been causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has. I’ve missed my walking group and book club, and the garden’s gotten away from me. Some friends at the senior center got upset because I keep bringing everything back to the memorial display.\n\nMy wife says most of our days are just… her trying to calm me down or stop me from making another call, planning another trip, or getting into another argument about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things for the memorial, or would you say you want to do them?\n\nPatient: I’d say I want to… mostly. It’s for my brother, so it feels important. But it’s gotten bigger than I meant. I spend most days on songs, calls, displays, even planning trips for it, and then everything else sort of gets pushed aside.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to control how others remember your brother through this memorial?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about controlling how they remember him. I just… what brings me in is that it’s gotten bigger than I meant. I’m spending most days chasing down songs, calling people, planning displays and even trips. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden, my walking group… most everything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about the memorial even when you're trying to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. It jumps around on me. I’ll try to do something else, and then it’s the display case, or maybe going to find a cousin, or some line from a ballad. I can’t really slow it down enough to put it in order.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there's something wrong with you if the memorial isn't perfect?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d put it that way. I just… I want it done right. If it isn’t, it feels like I let him down, maybe. But “something wrong with me”… I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing all this for the memorial because you genuinely believe it's what your brother would have wanted, or because you feel like you owe it to him?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate those two. Maybe a little of both. I don’t really know what he would’ve wanted, not exactly. I just feel like… if I don’t do it, then I didn’t do enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you're not working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes. I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m guilty,” exactly. It’s more like I get uneasy if I’m not doing something for it. Then I try not to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could stop working on the memorial if you felt like you had done enough, or do you feel like you have to keep going no matter what?\n\nPatient: I guess… I don’t know where “enough” is. It started as a simple memorial, but now I’m spending most days on it—songs, calling people, planning displays, even trips. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and all that. I keep thinking I just need to get one more thing right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing all this work for the memorial because you couldn't live with yourself if it wasn't done?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d put it that strongly. I just… I get this bad feeling when I stop, like I’m leaving something unfinished. I try not to think past that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're driven to keep working on the memorial because you're trying to make it perfect, or because you feel obligated to your brother?\n\nPatient: Maybe both, I don’t know. It feels like I keep needing to do one more thing for him.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be able to stop working on the memorial if someone you trusted told you it was already perfect as it is?\n\nPatient: Maybe… if I really trusted them. It’s hard to just stop, even if someone says it’s enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever consider the memorial complete if it wasn't perfect?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. Perfect’s a hard word. She says I’ve let the garden and my walking group and other things slide.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like every detail of the memorial has to be just right?\n\nPatient: I suppose so, yeah. It started as just a memorial, but now I keep chasing down songs, calling people, figuring out displays… even trips tied to it. Most days it takes over, and my wife says I’ve let the garden and walking group slip.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like if anything is not exactly as you envision it for the memorial, it would be a failure?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d call it a failure. I just… I want it done properly. It matters, that’s all.\n\nDoctor: Has your focus on the memorial been causing problems in your relationships or daily life?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess it has. I’ve missed my walking group and book club, and the garden’s gotten away from me. Some friends at the senior center got upset because I keep bringing everything back to the memorial display.\n\nMy wife says most of our days are just… her trying to calm me down or stop me from making another call, planning another trip, or getting into another argument about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to do these things for the memorial, or would you say you want to do them?\n\nPatient: I’d say I want to… mostly. It’s for my brother, so it feels important. But it’s gotten bigger than I meant. I spend most days on songs, calls, displays, even planning trips for it, and then everything else sort of gets pushed aside.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're trying to control how others remember your brother through this memorial?\n\nPatient: I don’t know about controlling how they remember him. I just… what brings me in is that it’s gotten bigger than I meant. I’m spending most days chasing down songs, calling people, planning displays and even trips. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden, my walking group… most everything else.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself thinking about the memorial even when you're trying to focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I do. It jumps around on me. I’ll try to do something else, and then it’s the display case, or maybe going to find a cousin, or some line from a ballad. I can’t really slow it down enough to put it in order.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like there's something wrong with you if the memorial isn't perfect?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d put it that way. I just… I want it done right. If it isn’t, it feels like I let him down, maybe. But “something wrong with me”… I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing all this for the memorial because you genuinely believe it's what your brother would have wanted, or because you feel like you owe it to him?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I can separate those two. Maybe a little of both. I don’t really know what he would’ve wanted, not exactly. I just feel like… if I don’t do it, then I didn’t do enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you're not working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes. I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m guilty,” exactly. It’s more like I get uneasy if I’m not doing something for it. Then I try not to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could stop working on the memorial if you felt like you had done enough, or do you feel like you have to keep going no matter what?\n\nPatient: I guess… I don’t know where “enough” is. It started as a simple memorial, but now I’m spending most days on it—songs, calling people, planning displays, even trips. My wife says I’ve pushed aside the garden and my walking group and all that. I keep thinking I just need to get one more thing right.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing all this work for the memorial because you couldn't live with yourself if it wasn't done?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d put it that strongly. I just… I get this bad feeling when I stop, like I’m leaving something unfinished. I try not to think past that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're driven to keep working on the memorial because you're trying to make it perfect, or because you feel obligated to your brother?\n\nPatient: Maybe both, I don’t know. I just know it’s gotten bigger than I meant. I spend most days chasing songs, calling people, planning displays… even trips. My wife says I’ve let the garden and walking group go. It feels like I keep needing to do one more thing for him.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you would be able to stop working on the memorial if someone you trusted told you it was already perfect as it is?\n\nPatient: Maybe… if I really trusted them. But I don’t know. It’s just gotten so big now—most days I’m still chasing down songs or calling people or planning some display. Even trips. It’s hard to just stop, even if someone says it’s enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could ever consider the memorial complete if it wasn't perfect?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. Perfect’s a hard word. I just know when I try to call it done, I end up thinking of another song to find, or someone else to call, or some display to plan. It’s taken over more than I meant it to.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're driven to keep working on the memorial because you're afraid of what others might think if it's not perfect?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure it’s that. I don’t really think much about other people judging it… at least not that I’ve noticed. It’s more just hard to leave it alone.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're doing all this for the memorial because you genuinely believe it's what your brother would have wanted?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say for sure it’s what he would’ve wanted. I tell myself that sometimes. But mostly… it’s just gotten bigger than I meant. I’m spending most days on songs, calling people, planning displays, even trips, and my wife says I’ve let the garden and my walking group slide.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like the memorial needs to be perfect because that's the only way you can feel like you've done your job in honoring your brother?\n\nPatient: I don’t know. Maybe that’s part of it, but I haven’t really thought of it that way. I just… I keep feeling like there’s something else I should do, and it’s hard to stop.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

I just feel like… if I don’t do it, then I didn’t do enough.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty when you're not working on the memorial?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… maybe sometimes. I don’t sit there thinking, “I’m guilty,” exactly. It’s more like I get uneasy if I’m not doing something for it. Then I try not to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you could stop working on the memorial if you felt like you had done enough, or do you feel like you have to keep going no matter what?\n\nPatient: I guess… I don’t know where “enough” is. I don’t really think much about other people judging it… at least not that I’ve noticed.
